The boundaries at the top and at both ends are semi-permeable, so that gas can flow out, and the axis mirrors the variables. A passive tracer, the colour, holds the fraction of the density that comes from the hot medium, starting at unity around the cloud and at zero inside, and is advected like mass and energy with the monotonic transport of [van Leer](#van-leer-1977) (1977), to trace the condensation of hot gas onto the cloud. The Poisson equation of self-gravity is solved every tenth step, since the structure of the cloud changes slowly compared with its dynamical time. Building heat conduction into this tested code proved more complicated than expected (App. A). Conduction is computed apart from the hydrodynamics, with the semi-implicit scheme of 2.4.1, from a code for heat conduction that Tomek Plewa provided (acknowledgements).

### 3.3 Conduction in Flash after Sander and Hensler

[Sander and Hensler](#sander-hensler-2022) (2022, §4) use the public code Flash, which solves the Euler equations on an adaptively refined Cartesian grid with the piecewise parabolic method and the Poisson equation with a multigrid solver for arbitrary distributions of mass. They add saturated conduction as a semi-implicit update of the temperature by the method of Crank and Nicolson, split by direction with the order of the sweeps permuted as in the hydrodynamics, with the conductivity of Slavin and Cox and a slope limiter on the flux at steep gradients, as described in [Sander and Hensler](#sander-hensler-2021) (2021) (2.2.7). Heating and cooling, dissociation, ionisation and mass diffusion enter as semi-analytic source terms for a single fluid. A comparison with the analytic solution of pure conduction in three dimensions, in [Sander and Hensler](#sander-hensler-2021) (2021), and a study at a finer level of refinement test the implementation (App. A).

### 3.4 AthenaPK in the simulations of Brüggen, Scannapieco and Grete

[Brüggen, Scannapieco and Grete](#bruggen-et-al-2023) (2023, §3.1) leave Flash, the code of the earlier papers of their series, for the open-source code AthenaPK, at commit f80dab9c of [github.com/parthenon-hpc-lab/athenapk](https://github.com/parthenon-hpc-lab/athenapk). AthenaPK implements finite-volume schemes for hydrodynamics and MHD on Parthenon ([Grete et al.](#grete-et-al-2023) 2023), a framework for adaptive mesh refinement that is portable across hardware and based on Athena++ ([Stone et al.](#stone-et-al-2020) 2020), K-Athena ([Grete, Glines and O'Shea](#grete-et-al-2021) 2021) and the library Kokkos ([Edwards, Trott and Sunderland](#edwards-et-al-2014) 2014; [Trott et al.](#trott-et-al-2021) 2021). It runs with mesh refinement on graphics processors of several vendors, at more than 90 per cent parallel efficiency on up to 73,728 of them. The runs use an unsplit, second-order predictor–corrector integrator of two stages, reconstruction by PPM and the HLL solver of [Harten, Lax and van Leer](#harten-et-al-1983) (1983), which is simple but captures only the fast magnetosonic waves. A generalised Lagrange multiplier cleans the divergence of the field ([Dedner et al.](#dedner-et-al-2002) 2002), after [Mignone and Tzeferacos](#mignone-tzeferacos-2010) (2010), with the speed of the cleaning wave limited by the hyperbolic time step and a ratio of diffusive to advective time scales of 0.4 for the run at Mach 3.54 with $\chi_0 = 1000$ and 0.1 otherwise. Where the update would give a negative temperature or density, all fluxes of the cell are recomputed by a forward Euler step with first-order reconstruction and the local Lax–Friedrichs solver, which keeps the update positive without floors. The mesh is refined where the passive scalar of the cloud exceeds 0.01 and derefined where it stays below 0.001 throughout a block. The box of $3000 \times 1500 \times 1500$ pc has a root grid of $512 \times 256 \times 256$ cells in blocks of $32^3$, large enough that the flow around the cloud is unaffected and no gas leaves through the sides. Two levels of refinement give cells of 1.46 pc, about 68 per cloud radius, and after $1\,t_\mathrm{cc}$ the refinement is frozen to save cost. The equation of state is adiabatic with $\gamma = 5/3$, and cooling follows the table of [Schure et al.](#schure-et-al-2009) (2009) for solar metallicity, switched off below $10^4$ K. Conduction is integrated by RKL2 super-time-stepping (2.4.5).

The paper compares this set-up with the earlier ones ([Brüggen, Scannapieco and Grete](#bruggen-et-al-2023) 2023, §4.4). Their second paper took the smaller of the classical and the saturated flux, where the harmonic mean of eq. 15 now passes smoothly between them, integrated conduction implicitly in Flash, and used other cooling tables. Their third paper cleaned the divergence purely parabolically in Flash and reconstructed at second order, whereas PPM extends the spectral bandwidth and resolves more small-scale structure in the tail. The results of that paper are reproduced with PLM, and PPM gives a higher effective resolution and converges much better. The HLLD solver of [Miyoshi and Kusano](#miyoshi-kusano-2005) (2005), used before, heated isolated cells artificially and drove a numerical instability related to the carbuncle of [Peery and Imlay](#peery-imlay-1988) (1988) in these supersonic flows of strong contrast. The low-dissipation HLLD solver of [Minoshima and Miyoshi](#minoshima-miyoshi-2021) (2021) still failed in some runs, so that PPM with HLL proved best for effective resolution and stability, at the cost of an unresolved contact discontinuity. **[GK]** At commit f80dab9c, of 14 January 2022 on a branch for super-time-stepping, `src/hydro/diffusion/conduction.cpp` forms the anisotropic flux like `HeatFlux_aniso` of the C version of Athena (2.3.6). Its transverse gradients are limited with the MC limiter in `limiters::lim4`, and the field, the density and the diffusivity on a face are arithmetic means of the two adjacent cells. The diffusivity, however, is the smaller of the classical one and $0.34(P/\rho)^{3/2}/|\nabla T|$, a saturated flux of $0.34\rho c_\mathrm{iso}^3$ with $c_\mathrm{iso}$ the isothermal sound speed of the gas. This is the minimum that §4.4 attributes to the earlier paper, not the harmonic mean of eq. 15. For ionised gas of solar composition, with $\mu \approx 0.6$ and $n_\mathrm{e}/n \approx 0.52$, eq. 3 corresponds to about $5.9\rho c_\mathrm{iso}^3$, so that the saturated flux of this commit is about 17 times weaker. The harmonic mean, with the saturated flux taken upwind after [Mignone et al.](#mignone-et-al-2012) (2012, App. A) and an adjustable prefactor, entered the main branch of AthenaPK only in August 2024 (pull request 1, opened in October 2022). Which version the runs used thus remains open.
